# Seed 40 on the AMD Ryzen 7 PRO 8840HS - the batch's first run: the JIT genes, half the time of the best before (Iteration 173)

`seed 40 tag2 9h then experiment compare-fronts.py --rounds 6 --frontier
--cpus 2,8`, commit 23e66f3a (Iteration 169, the after-seed-39 batch
applied), started 04:34: the fronts of seeds 36-39 carried in, population
256, 136 generations before the deadline (seed 39: 82 in 7 hours); deaths
1,656 at the reach limit and 5 with a wrong corpus - new designs all, two of
them a jitloop mutation (ee0eff671a with varslot, 8dff2a3647 with tracer);
no carried design died. The chain took 8 h 39 min of its 9 hours.

Session 35 measured it with seeds 36-39 (calibration 0.988 on cpu 2, 1.008 on
cpu 8): **not small** - fastest 0.187 (d9247d19b0: jit, jitloop, jitinl)
against 0.280, the best carried; smallest 17,762 bytes against 17,770; memory
152 KB against 148. 89 of the joint front's 97 designs are seed 40's. Against
the best carried design, the fastest's bubble is 24% of its ratio (0.084
against 0.348) and ackermann 58% (0.452 against 0.781); loop and fib about the
same. Every design is ~2.9 KB smaller than before the batch (nolibc, the tight
ELF), less the ~170 bytes the standard's numbers and CREATE-FILE added.

Against the predictions written before the run (results/predictions.md,
Iterations 154, 166, 169): all right but one only roughly - the images grew
~170 bytes, not 100-150.

The run's report:

# Evolved VM designs

34983 designs evaluated, 33322 alive. Speed is the geometric mean of median cpu time / hand-made s6, long workloads (Iteration 100) over kernel, fib, corpus, loop, bubble, ackermann,
relative to s6-cv8b; size is the self-hosting image, binary the stripped engine. sieve, parse is held out.
The front is over three objectives, equal (Iteration 75): speed, binary + image, peak memory - memory within 8 KB a tie (Iteration 77).
